As built (`plan-change.service.ts`, approver ≠ requester; only `platform-owner` holds `system/plan:approve-change`). Go-live creates two Owners (SA-7-39). SA-03 edge case (line 771) stands.

(2) Rolling back a published ExpatReady email: Platform Owner only (closes SA-07 OQ-11). (17) SA-09 Q2 closed — Trial / Active / Payment hold / Security lock + 5 module counts. (18) SA-10 Q1 closed — no "Immigration content" category (routing parked, R12). - **W-4 — Deletion warnings recipients (the product owner, 12 Oct; closes SA-04 §13 Q9):** the day 30 / 60 / 97 deletion warnings go to **billing email + Owner + Admins** (R30 set 2), like every other billing notice. Recipients change in SA-02 and SA-04.
- **W-5 — Owner-action billing emails (the product owner, 12 Oct; closes SA-04 §13 Q12):** "Upgrade charge failed" and "Subscription started early" follow the one R30 rule — **billing email + Owner + Admins**; SA-05 Appendix B updated to match (SA4-X18).

From October you'll pay $149/month." No email at a monthly ↔ yearly switch (confirm screen + plan-change email cover it). SA-4-90 updated.
- **W-7 — Plan-screen register suggestions (the product owner, 12 Oct; closes SA-03 §13 Q7):** keep as specified — the "New — needs a decision" list with reminders (SA-3-38), the side-by-side plan comparison (SA-3-33), and the built `system/plan:*` keys (`role-template.platform.ts:83-98`; data-foundation rule).
- **W-8 — "Stop data erasure" flag details (the product owner, 12 Oct; completes SA-12 Q18):** key follows the built catalogue naming (e.g. `governance.disposal` — developer confirms the convention); **does not cover client records** (pauses the background job only; no read-only mode, screens unaffected); default off, platform-wide only (W-3(8)). While on, approvals and grace periods continue; nothing is erased; some erasures may complete after the 30-day due date (reminder only, G-Q1).
- **W-9 — Permission naming (the product owner, 12 Oct; closes SA-11 §13 Q6):** `system/*`, as built; the old `global/*` name is retired. No change needed.
- **W-10 — Writer-inferred wordings confirmed (the product owner, 12 Oct):** (1) the "Deletion outcome" email (SA-11 Q19) goes to the person who made the request to ExpatReady; (2) the permission for the Escalation and At-risk settings is decided in the AG review (AG-11 / AG-16); (3) ExpatReady's e-sign certificate wording is published by the Content publisher, like the default consent text (Q16, R9-26).
- **W-11 — Erasure switch direction (the product owner, 12 Oct; corrects W-3(8) / W-8 / R9-34 naming):** the flag is **"Data erasure"** (key e.g. `governance.disposal`), **on by default** — approved erasures run after the 7-day grace. Emergency stop = **trip the kill switch** on it, with a reason (audited): the flag reads off and erasure pauses ("Erasure paused by ExpatReady"); untrip to resume. Verified in code: a tripped kill switch resolves the flag to off, and an unreadable kill switch also resolves as tripped (`platform-flag/flag.service.ts:104-105`) — so a fault pauses erasure, the safe direction. Unchanged: platform-wide only, does not cover client records, screens unaffected.
- **W-12 — Owner floor for role removal (the product owner, 12 Oct):** one floor for both deactivation and removing the Owner role — **at least one active Owner**, as built (`platform-owner-guard.ts`, refuses zero). SA-07's "fewer than two" wording (SA7-D41, SA7-D56, §3.3, SA-7-25, BR-7, US-7) and the §12 "≥1 → ≥2" code-change item are removed.
- **W-13 — Security lock keys (the product owner, 12 Oct):** applying and lifting a security lock is **Platform Owner only** (SA7-D53). Remove `system/firm:suspend` and `system/firm:reinstate` (the security-lock lift key, `permission-catalog-rows.ts:108-110`) from `platform-admin` (`role-template.platform.ts:189-190`) + seed. Missed in SR-Q4; added to the SA-07 code-differences list and the developer tasks.
- **W-14 — Support reads the audit log (the product owner, 12 Oct):** keep the code — `platform-support` holds `system/audit-log:list` (`role-template.platform.ts:231`), read-only, every read audited. SA-07 Appendix A "Audit trail — read" gains the Support tick.
- **W-15 — What a firm may hide (the product owner, 12 Oct; closes SA1-Q3, SA1B-Q2; refines Q17(2)):** firms may hide ExpatReady's standard checklist items and standard steps **without** a deadline. **Never hideable:** anything with a deadline (any kind) and **all government steps**. Per case, staff may mark any item "not applicable". Developer check: whether the built firm layer (`pack-tenant-extension.service.ts`) enforces this.
- **W-16 — No reordering of standard steps (the product owner, 12 Oct; closes SA1B-Q5):** order comes from the pack; it is a guide, not a lock — staff may work in any order. A firm's own order lives in its to-do list templates (map §10). Matches SA-06 `agent/case:configure-pack` ("no reordering").
- **W-17 — Firms see only the output of ExpatReady's configuration (the product owner, 12 Oct; reverses Q18, R26, R9-1 and SA1-D9's firm-wide extra items):** SA-01B configuration (conditions, field groups, scoring, form mapping) is ExpatReady's and never shown to firms — firms see only the **final questionnaire** and the **dynamic document checklist** for the case's programme, category and family. **Withdrawn:** firm-added questions (Q18, R24 — not built); hiding / reordering optional questions (Q18); the "who answers" switch (R26 — not built; staff already fill answers on the client's behalf on the case); the questionnaire-layer screens (R9-1); firm-wide extra checklist items (SA1-D9, SA-1-34, SA-1-66 firm-wide wording). **Kept:** per-case additions as built (`PackTenantExtension` kind `CHECKLIST_ADDITION`, stored per firm + client + case pin — `client-spine.prisma:567-585`) = "Request a document" on a case; hiding standard items without a deadline (W-15) and renaming a step's client name (decision 16, SR-Q9) are unchanged. Firm-specific facts every client is asked (e.g. referral source) belong on the lead / client record (AG-02 / AG-07). Knock-ons: SA-01, SA-01B (SA-1B-93, SA-1B-99, SA1B-D38, SA1-D9, SA-1-34, SA-1-66), SA-06 keys if any; AG-08 (clone-and-customise and firm questions withdrawn), AG-07 / AG-09 (per-case additions) — AG review.
- **W-18 — Plan on firm creation; firm changes its own plan (the product owner, 12 Oct):** staff "New firm" route stays **as built** — staff pick the plan (`firm-provisioning.ts:378` requires it), **no pre-selection**; sign-up links unchanged (firm picks on the sign-up page, SA2-X1). **During a trial** the Owner may switch freely to any public plan — no charge, trial end date unchanged; after the trial the normal upgrade / downgrade rules apply (SA-04 §3.7, SA4-D28). New rule for SA-04 (SA4-D29) and SA-02; trial plan-switch is a small build (developer list).
- **W-19 — "Extend trial" default (the product owner, 12 Oct):** pre-filled **7 days**, editable; reason required; whole trial ≤ 90 days (SA4-D29).
- **W-20 — Firm reminder timings without values in SA-05 (the product owner, 12 Oct):** document / questionnaire chase cadence, booking reminder timing per appointment type and the five AG-16 timings are marked "**defaults set in the AG review**" (AG-04, AG-08, AG-09, AG-16) and added to the AG review list.
- **W-21 — Full-read audit fixes (the product owner approved the text-fix batch, 12 Oct):** mechanical corrections from the four read-only audits applied as same-day revisions; rulings already made: overdue list = Platform Owner and Billing (SA-07 Appendix A); a discount is applied by Billing or Owner (not the account manager); each staff member connects their own calendar and sets their own availability (R16) — firm-wide calendar configuration stays Owner / Admin; `system/mail-template:publish` stays a separate Owner-only key, distinct from `system/template:publish` (SR-Q2, as built); breach key name `system/breach:manage` (Platform Owner only); firm reason on role changes optional except the Owner role (R36 / SR-Q7); dormant firm keys (`agent/legal-hold:manage`, `agent/breach:manage`, `agent/retention-policy:manage`) not shown as active firm capabilities.

## AG-01 review — decisions (the product owner, 13 Oct)

- **AG01-1 — One firm address:** the client portal lives at the firm's SA-02 web address (`maple.expatready.com`, SA2-D8), shown **read-only** in Branding ("To change it, contact support"). AG-01's firm-editable "portal subdomain" (L232, AG-1-7, branding task) is removed. Built: `firm.controller.ts` `POST firms/:firmId/slug` (Super Admin). Custom domains: separate question.
- **AG01-2 — Setup checklist gates nothing (the product owner, 13 Oct):** end state renamed **"Setup complete"** — a progress marker, not a gate; "Create your first client" is never locked. Order: **licence first**, highlighted "Needed before your first retainer"; firm profile pre-filled from sign-up (usually already done). Optional, nudged: payment account (SA-Q1), team invites, branding, firm layout, consent screen — each says what it unlocks. Real gates only at the point of action: card payment needs a connected account; a retainer needs a licensed person. Replaces AG-1-37 / AG-1-38 "READY unlocks…" and "payment rail required" (L329-343). Onboarding is frontend mock only (`firm-setup/*`).
- **AG01-3 — Two-step sign-in is optional (the product owner, 13 Oct):** never forced by default — for firm staff and for clients. Built single switch kept ("Require two-step sign-in for all staff"), **default OFF**; the Owner may switch it on for the whole firm; any staff member may turn it on for themselves at any time (built: TOTP, passkeys, recovery codes — `auth/firm/firm-account.controller.ts`). Client portal switch: default OFF (matches R17). "Admins only" level dropped. **Code change:** the built default when no policy row exists is mandatory (`identity-auth.prisma:283-293`; `auth/mfa-policy/mfa-policy.service.ts:69`) → make the default **not mandatory** for both pools. ExpatReady's own staff are unaffected (mandatory, SA-07). Replaces AG-1-40 / Q4 three-level model.
- **AG01-3a — Optional checklist item (the product owner, 13 Oct):** the setup checklist includes an optional **"Turn on two-step sign-in"** item (explains the benefit; never required).
- **AG01-4 — Mixed representative types in one firm (the product owner, 13 Oct):** allowed (replaces FD-31 "one profession per firm", BR-11). Each licensed person records representative type, licensing body and number on their own profile (Q24).
- **AG01-5 — Lead representative drives retainer and forms; firm default (the product owner, 13 Oct):** one concept — the **case's lead representative** (licensed-only, decision 13) is used on the retainer **and** on the IRCC representative form (IMM 5476), so they are always the same person. New firm setting **"Default lead representative for new cases"** — default **none (chosen per case)**, or one named licensed person (e.g. "Always Client A"); placement **Advanced**; staff may change it per case. The retainer lists others on the case team as "people who will assist". The firm-level "designated person" card and its two flags (`isAuthorizedRepresentative` / `isResponsiblePerson`) are **dropped**. Research basis (not legal advice, rule 7): CICC Code SOR/2022-128 s. 24 — the service agreement is between the client and one licensee, with name and registration number, and names of persons likely to assist; IMM 5476 names one individual representative with membership ID, no firm field. Knock-ons: AG-01 §3.5, AG-1-30..32, BR-11/12, §7; AG-05 (retainer signer, assisting list); AG-07 (default lead representative on case creation); AG-16 (setting).
- **AG01-5a — No licence monitoring confirmed (the product owner, 13 Oct):** AG1-D19 stands — no expiry date stored, no validity checks, reminders or register lookups; a person is licensed until the firm removes the licence from their profile. AG1-D8 stands (a deactivated staff member's licence is never used on new documents). The AG1-D19 "uses" list drops "who approves AI-written client text" and "who supervises unlicensed staff" (R5).
- **AG01-6 — Scope of the "no licensed person" rule (the product owner, 13 Oct):** **retainer: blocked** until an active staff member has a licence on their profile ("No active licensed consultant — add one"; kept exception, rule 7); **government forms: never blocked** to prepare — **warning at submission** only ("No licensed representative on this case. Add one before submitting.", decision 13). Replaces AG-1-51 / BR-18 "retainers and forms" block.
- **AG01-7 — One last-Owner rule (the product owner, 13 Oct):** SA-6-44's only-active-Owner guard replaces AG-01's separate "last admin / RBAC-admin permission" guard (AG1-D15, AG-1-22, BR-5): one rule, one message ("This is your firm's only active Owner. Give the Owner role to another person first."). Fix the built bug in the same work: only an Owner gives the Owner role (SA6-D3; `role-assignment.service.ts:141-158`).
- **AG01-8 — Work handover when someone leaves: one-click, no-brainer (the product owner, 13 Oct; replaces AG-1-48 per-type picker):** one screen at deactivation, never blocking the lock-out (AG1-D2). **Main choice:** "Hand all open work to ___", **pre-selected smart suggestion** = the colleague who shares the most of the leaver's open cases in any slot (case team, AG-07 §3.8), shown as "Client C — already on 6 of Client H's 8 cases"; fallback the Owner. **Slots:** case officer and reviewer → the chosen person (reviewer cleared if the case is not in review); **lead representative** → the chosen person if licensed, else the firm's default lead representative (AG01-5), else "Needs a lead representative". Tasks follow their case / lead; personal tasks follow the main choice. **Appointments** move if the new person is free (client gets "Booking updated"), else "Needs a new host" — never silently cancelled. A preview line shows the result before confirming. Optional, collapsed: "Choose per type", "Skip for now" (→ "Needs an owner" list). No new settings. Depends on the AG-07 case-team model (not built). Frontend mock's "block until handed over" is removed.
- **AG01-9 — Firm profile pre-filled, task 1 starts complete (the product owner, 13 Oct):** name, address, Owner contact from SA-02 creation; language English (Canada) or French if chosen at sign-up; timezone from the address's province, else America/Toronto; **default currency for new cases CAD** (changeable per case, R18). Reporting currency and FX source removed (R18). Built: Owner legal-profile route and screen (`settings/components/profile-api.ts`).
- **AG01-10 — Offices hidden until a second office (the product owner, 13 Oct):** a firm has one office automatically and never sees "office". Owner or Admin adds one in **Settings → Advanced → Offices → "Add an office"**; from then the office field shows in invites, staff profiles and filters, and per-office invoice numbering becomes available (R18). Removing the second office hides it again. `officeId` stays on records from day one (data-foundation rule). Not built (frontend mock only).
- **AG01-11 — Firm step-up on two high-stakes actions (the product owner, 13 Oct; replaces AG-1-42 "fast-follow"):** before **giving or removing the Owner role** and **connecting or changing the payment account**, the person confirms identity — **authenticator code or passkey if they use two-step sign-in, otherwise re-enter their password**; valid 5 minutes (built window). Plus the built use (changing your own two-step settings). Everyday actions, incl. editing your own licence, have no step-up. No setting. Built today: firm step-up with TOTP / WebAuthn only (`auth/firm/firm-self-mfa.controller.ts` `POST step-up`); to build: password re-entry path + wiring the two actions.
- **AG01-12 — Passkeys in Release 1 (the product owner, 13 Oct; built stays):** firm staff choosing two-step sign-in are offered **passkey** (device fingerprint / face / PIN, or phone via QR) or **authenticator app**; recovery codes for a lost device. Built: `auth/firm/firm-account.controller.ts`. Replaces AG-01 "fast-follow" (L351, AG-1-40, §13).
- **AG01-13 — Licence records: log changes, not views (the product owner, 13 Oct):** every add, change or removal of a licence is audited (who, when, before / after); viewing is not logged (the number is printed on every retainer). Replaces "every view/edit audited" (L310, BR-10, BR-16, AG-1-46).
- **AG01-14 — Custom portal domain → Release 2 (the product owner, 13 Oct):** wish-list, not day-to-day (firm branding shows everywhere in Release 1); kept in the SA-03 catalogue (#23) marked "Release 2 — coming later". Knock-ons: SA-03 #23 note, AG-15-27 release, SA2-D8 wording.
- **AG01-15 — Stalled-setup reminders (the product owner, 13 Oct):** two only — 3 and 7 days after the last setup progress — email + dashboard card, fixed ExpatReady wording naming the next step ("You're 1 step from set-up complete: add your licence (needed before your first retainer)"); stop when setup is complete or on "Don't remind me". Code constant, no setting.
- **AG01-16 — Screen ownership (the product owner, 13 Oct):** the firm's "Allow support to make changes" toggle (R9-pass ruling 2; built backend `firm-support-access.ts:93-110`) and the firm "Get help" ticket screen (SA-10) are specified in **AG-16** (Settings). AG-01 keeps only the Owner-only action list.
- **AG01-17 — Role preview at invite (the product owner, 13 Oct):** kept in the invite form and role editor, worded with the same ~30 plain-language capabilities as the role editor (R2), never permission keys. Frontend mock exists (`settings/role-management/role-capability-preview.tsx`).
- **AG01-18 — Multi-row invite (the product owner, 13 Oct):** one invite form with rows (name, email, one or more roles with the AG01-17 preview), **"Case Officer" pre-selected** per row, "+ Add another person"; seat check before sending ("7 of 10 seats after these invites"; over the limit → "remove 1 person or upgrade", nothing half-sent); duplicates / existing staff flagged on the row; each person gets their own invite (built route, one call per row). No spreadsheet upload. Opens from the setup checklist (three empty rows) and Settings → Team (one row).
- **AG01-19 — AG-01 text-fix batch approved (the product owner, 13 Oct):** the 20 decided knock-ons (only-Owner guard; reason only for the Owner role; custom-role delete replacement only if left with no role; capability role editor + Advanced; R37 subscription-invoice view; neutral licence wording and person-held licences; retainer uses the case's lead representative; "Lead representative" rename; R5 removals; R18 removals; payment account on every plan via hosted sign-up; rule 7 clean-up; staff account emails ExpatReady-owned, none on suspend / deactivate / role change; fixed-wording onboarding notices; checklist items firm layout + consent screen; starter-role approval Platform Owner or Admin; support toggle per ruling 2 (screen in AG-16); stale text); align §7 role model to SA-06 (`FirmUserRole`, several roles per person); one branding entity `FirmBranding` (AG-15 refers to it); Agent Overview AI-approval line removed; **§12 rewritten against ERv2 code**; new developer tasks; write AG-01 v1.19; pending rows for AG-05, AG-07, AG-15, AG-16, SA-03.
- **AG01-17 refinement (the product owner, 13 Oct):** in the invite form, **one always-visible line under each row's role** (e.g. "Case Officer — cases, documents, client messages · no billing or settings"), plus a **"Compare roles"** link opening a side panel with the six starter roles side by side (can / cannot). The role editor keeps the full capability preview. Same capability list (R2); no extra build.
- **AG01-20 — Stored signature, automatic (the product owner, 13 Oct; closes AG-01 §13 Q10):** each representative adds their signature once (upload or draw), prompted in the setup licence step and kept in **My profile → Signature**, with one tick "Use my signature on documents I'm the lead representative for". When staff **Send for signature**, the case's lead representative's stored signature is **already applied** — the client signs in the portal, done; no per-send choice, no firm setting. No signature yet → the send screen offers "Add your signature now" or "Sign electronically instead" (e-signature, SA8-D21, stays available). Same for letters and government forms where the lead representative signs. Background safeguards (no extra steps): only the person uploads / changes / deletes their own signature; every use audited; the signing certificate states it factually ("Client A's signature applied, sent by Client H, 14 Mar"); a deactivated person's signature is never used (AG1-D8). Knock-ons: AG-05 (send-for-signature applies the stored signature), SA-08 (certificate wording names an applied stored signature), AG-07 / forms, AG-12 (document templates' signer line).
- **AG01-21 — Handover leftovers stay visible (the product owner, 13 Oct; closes AG-01 §13 Q11):** skipped work goes to the **existing Unassigned lists** (AG1-D7) — no second list — each item tagged "**From <name>**" and filterable in one click (so it does not mix unseen with new enquiries). A dashboard **"Needs attention — from <name> (left <date>)"** card groups the three leftovers (need an owner · need a lead representative · appointment needs a new host), one action per group, clearing itself as items are assigned and disappearing when done. Knock-on: AG-14 dashboard card; AG-02 / AG-07 list tag and filter.
- **AG01-22 — "Setup complete" = licence + firm profile (the product owner, 13 Oct; closes AG-01 §13 Q12):** optional items (payment account, team invites, branding, firm layout, consent screen, two-step sign-in, signature) do not count; after completion they stay as a dismissible "Make the most of ExpatReady" list, each saying what it unlocks. The signature is offered in the licence step but optional (AG01-20).
- **AG01-23 — Signature at the moment of sending (the product owner, 13 Oct):** on the send screen of a retainer **or any form / letter** the lead representative signs, if they have no stored signature: **(a) the representative is sending** → an inline **"Add your signature"** box right there (draw or upload) with **"Save for next time"** ticked by default (saves to My profile → Signature with consent), or "Sign electronically instead"; **(b) someone else is sending** → "Client A hasn't added a signature. Send anyway — Client A will be asked to e-sign after the client signs"; it goes out at once and Client A gets one "Countersign Client C's retainer" notification; nothing waits. The **"Needs attention — from <name>"** dashboard card is shown to **Owner and Admins**.

- **AG01-24 — Times shown in the item's own office time, labelled (the product owner, 18 Oct; closes AG-01 Q15).** Home: AG-01 (AG-1-74 rule 5). Epics affected: AG-01, AG-04, AG-11, AG-16. With two or more offices, a time tied to an office (booking, case deadline, office clock) is shown in that office's time zone and labelled when it differs from the viewer's: "10:00 (Calgary)"; no conversion. Items with no office of their own (e.g. personal tasks) use the viewer's office zone. Why: staff quote times to clients of that office; a converted time invites the wrong time being told.
- **AG01-25 — Deactivate screen pointer words (the product owner, 18 Oct; closes AG-01 Q16(a)).** Home: AG-01 (AG-1-75). "[Name]'s open work is handed over below." — pointing to the one-click handover on the same screen (AG01-8).
- **AG01-26 — "Connected by" field (the product owner, 18 Oct; closes AG-01 Q16(b)).** Home: AG-10 (`MailboxConnection.connectedByUserId`, `AG-10…:822`). Epics affected: AG-01 (AG-1-75 data line).
- **AG01-27 — Office add / edit uses `agent/settings:edit` (the product owner, 18 Oct; closes AG-01 Q16(c)).** Home: AG-01 (AG01-10). Epics affected: AG-01, SA-06 (key description gains "offices"). Owner and Admin (`SA-06…:976`); no new key. **AG-01 open questions all closed (18 Oct).**
- **AG01-28 — Team list wording and who sees it (the product owner, 18 Oct; closes AG-01 Q17).** Home: AG-01 (AG-1-76). Broken connection: "Email: disconnected" / "Calendar: disconnected" (AG-16-16 wording); deactivated person: "(Inactive) Name" (AG-04 BR-20 label). Settings → Team is visible to holders of `agent/staff:manage` (Owner, Admin); colleague names in pickers and on records need no key. Epics affected: AG-01.

## Product-wide rules from 18 Oct (X-series)

- **X-1 — Documents written to be understood, by people and AI (the product owner, 18 Oct).** Home: Agent `00-Overview` → "Product rules every epic follows"; writing detail in `WRITER-BRIEF`. Epics affected: all (applied as each epic is reviewed; AG-07 first, as the pilot). The product owner: "the epic documents are very big and the developer gets cognitive overload … most of them may not be native English speakers … use clear language … explain the jargon … but developers use AI (Claude Code), so it should be AI-friendly too … use examples where possible, like a really detailed BRD, so they have everything to code themselves or with AI." Evidence: AG epics total ~568,000 words (AG-07 ~57,000, AG-11 ~64,000); AG-10 carries 102 inline "changed" markers; struck-through withdrawn text sits in the build path, which an AI agent can mis-read as live. Rules: (1) **Each epic file in this order:** Start here (purpose in 5 lines, key terms with one example each, main flow diagram, build order) → build cards (exact, self-contained, code names in brackets) → rules and tests → appendix (decision table, history, closed questions). (2) **Plain English for non-native readers:** one idea per sentence, about 20 words; simple words; every term defined on first use and in the glossary; one word per concept (controlled word list); examples before rules; worked examples wherever possible (BRD depth). (3) **No history or struck text in the build path** (moved to the appendix; nothing deleted). (4) **Key terms copied from the glossary by script**, never retyped. (5) **Checks on every rewrite:** script confirms every requirement ID, number, permission key and field name unchanged; a second reviewer compares meaning card by card; sentence-length and word-list checks; an AI test (Claude Code plans one card from Start here + the card). (6) Rewrite happens during each epic's review, never as a bulk rewrite. AG-07 is written in full in this format after its decisions are made (pilot).

- **X-2 — The people model: one person record per real person; one place for their details (option A; the product owner, 18 Oct; replaces AG07-D5).** Home: AG-02 (owns the person record, AG7-D9). Epics affected: AG-02, AG-07, AG-08, AG-10, AG-15 / CP-01, AG-17, AG-18, SA-11; AG-03, AG-04, AG-06, AG-13 (added 18 Oct after the cross-epic review: they still named the retired Contact model); Doc-1 (`ERv2-sanitized/Docs/03-Doc-1-Platform-Foundation.md:1233, :1252`). Evidence (code check, 18 Oct): Party is the built identity (`client-spine.prisma:20`); consent, erasure, legal hold, keys and export are keyed to Party; `AnswerSubject.partyId` optional, null only "not-yet-a-Party" (`:212-221`); answer store checks firm only, not party equality (`answer-store.service.ts:669-682`); portal access is a separate grant — only an active `ClientCustomer` login or `CORPORATE_SEAT` (`infra/10-portal-subject-fns.sql:57-82`), so a child's record gives no login or emails; keys minted lazily (`key-hierarchy.service.ts:414-453`); no Contact table built; answers' access, encryption, export and erasure key on the answer's Party (`answer-store.service.ts:673-675, 728-734`; `export-aggregator.service.ts:382, 402`). Rules: (1) **Every real person in a case gets their own person record (Party, kind PERSON)**, created automatically when a family member is added by staff or the questionnaire; before creating, ExpatReady offers the client's existing family members ("Client J, daughter, already on file — use her?"); staff decide (AG16-44); merge stays the repair path. A record with no person (`AnswerSubject` without Party) only for someone who does not exist yet (unborn child); it gets a record once the person exists. (2) **A person is a person; the role is per filing:** principal / spouse / child / other is stored on the applicant of each filing (`CaseApplicant`, keyed to the Party), so one person keeps one set of answers across all their cases. (3) **Answers live under the person they describe**; privacy requests (export, erasure) work per person. (4) **Visibility:** the principal sees the answers of family members on the cases they share (minors always; principal handles the family, CP1-D9); an adult's answers are shared with the principal by default; staff may tick **"Keep private from the principal"** on an adult's answers. New rule to build: principal sees family members on a shared case. (5) **One place for a person's details:** name, email, phone, language, country on the built person details (`PartyProfile`, extended with AG-02's fields such as phone region, country of origin, time zone); sales/client fields (lifecycle stage, file owner, client code, office) on a small separate one-per-person client-file record; consent in the built `ConsentRecord`. "Contact" is the name of the screen, not a second table; AG-02's `Contact`, AG-08's `ContactFamilyMember` / `ContactProfile` / `principalContactId` are retired in favour of Party + `PartyRelationship` + `AnswerSubject`. AG-17 employer-contact roles point at Party. (6) The UCI and every per-person government number are answers about that person (AG16-73), so letters naming a family member's UCI match to that person and their cases. Backend lead decides: exact field placement, the principal-sees-family rule, data migration (none built). Status: approved, not yet applied.

### AG review order (the product owner, 13 Oct)
AG-01 ✅ → AG-16 → AG-07 → AG-02 → AG-03 → AG-04 → AG-05 → AG-06 → AG-08 → AG-09 → AG-10 → AG-11 → AG-12 → AG-13 → AG-15 (+CP-01) → AG-14 → AG-18 → AG-17. Knock-ons are applied in each module's own review (option A); the dev team does not start an AG epic before its review.

## Product decisions AG16-1 – AG16-104 (the product owner, 14–18 Oct; started in the AG-16 review)

> **Read this first (18 Oct).** These decisions began in the AG-16 review, but from about AG16-24 they apply across the product. The ID prefix "AG16" is historical and does **not** mean "AG-16 only"; IDs are kept because the documents cite them. **Type:** *Product-wide rule* = applies to every epic (home: Agent `00-Overview`, "Product rules every epic follows"); *Cross-epic* = changes several epics; *One epic* = changes one. **Epics** = documents that cite the decision (generated 18 Oct from the latest versions). Each epic lists the decisions that change it in its own table "Decisions that change this epic". From 18 Oct, new decisions are numbered per epic review (e.g. AG07-D1) or X-n for product-wide rules, each with "Home:" and "Epics affected:" lines.

### Decisions in full

*(original heading: "AG-16 review — decisions (the product owner, 14 Oct)")*

- **AG16-1 — Settings structure (approved):** nine sections — 1 Your firm (profile, language, timezone, default currency, business hours & holidays) · 2 How clients see you (logo, colours, sender name, footer & licence text, email signature, read-only portal address, live preview) · 3 Team · 4 Getting paid (payment account, invoice numbering, tax, "Due in N days", offline payment instructions, invoice footer) · 5 Connections (firm mailbox, WhatsApp, video account; other provider collapsed) · 6 Client messages & AI (automatic messages on/off, one Reminders page, AI switch — phase to confirm) · 7 Security & your data (two-step switch, activity log, export all firm data) · 8 Help & support · 9 Plan & billing (subscription, extras on). **Set up your tools** links (Leads, Booking, Templates, Client portal, Reports, Import — each list lives in its feature screen). **Advanced:** offices, default lead representative, deadlines, standard items, legal settings, developer API. **My account:** profile & language, licence, stored signature, email signature, calendar, availability, out-of-office, notifications, two-step sign-in, sessions. AG-01 is the single spec home for profile and branding. Pending rulings: AI phase, IP allow-list, business-hours default. Code (frontend pull 14 Oct): templates already moved to `/app/templates` (aligned); settings tabs still include "questionnaires" (conflicts W-17) and a mock "plan" tab (duplicate) — to remove; new "Legal declarations" tab — ruling needed.
- **AG16-2 — "Legal declarations" tab: remove (option A).** The frontend tab added 13 Oct (`src/features/settings/profile/legal-declarations/*`, practice and privacy jurisdiction, both required and empty "nothing may default") is not in any spec or decision. No decided feature reads either value. It contradicts Rule 9 (zero setup, defaults first) and rule 7 (ExpatReady never decides which regulator or privacy law applies). The firm's province, from its address (AG01-9), already drives tax and timezone. Frontend task: remove the tab and its mock. No backend exists. Any future need comes through a decision first.
- **AG16-3 — AI Doers stay in Release 1 (option B).** All four AI Doers — Reader, Sorter, Checker and Drafter — stay Release 1 as decided (AG11-D4, 5 Oct scope review), built but locked off until Super Admin records the Canadian provider (AG11-D13, D15); the Owner turns them on with one switch in Settings section 6. The product owner's ruling: the Drafter is not treated as the "AI-written content" wish-list example of the 11 Oct principle. SR-Q10 (approving AI-written client text uses the sending permission) stays Release 1. Code (14 Oct): no AI Doer and no AI/OCR provider code exists; the built `drafter` capability (`packages/contracts/src/schemas/capability.ts:264-269`, flag "document generation") is the non-AI document generator — name clash, see AG16-4.
- **AG16-4 — Doer family and the "Drafter" name clash (option A).** One family, "Doers": 14 everyday (non-AI) Doers — the glossary's 12 plus Qualifier and Finder below *(count corrected 14 Oct; was "12")* — always on with no firm set-up, and 4 AI Doers carrying an **AI** badge behind the single AI switch. The AI provider is not a Doer; it is recorded by ExpatReady in SA-08 and is never seen by the firm. **The code's `drafter` stays the non-AI document generator** (built, `capability.ts:264-269`, `feature.docs.generate`); **the AI writer is renamed "Writer"** in every document (glossary, AG-10, AG-11, AG-16, SA docs). Glossary to add the built Doers **Qualifier** (lead scoring, `capability.ts:202`) and **Finder** (firm-wide search, `capability.ts:307`), and to note that **Reminder** and **Messenger** have no separate code entry yet (Messenger exists only as the SMS and WhatsApp sub-parts, `capability.ts:221-229`). No code rename.
- **AG16-5 — IP allow-list: not in Release 1, parked on the wish list (option A).** AG-16 v1.15 §3 line 302 (optional IP allow-list with self-lockout guard, BR-10) and the "IP guard at the edge" in the data flow (line 309) are removed from Release 1. Not built (spec marks it NEW; the only IP allow-list in code is the infrastructure `TRUST_PROXY` note, `build-helmet-options.ts:63`, unrelated). Reason: Rule 8 (park depth); staff work from home and phones, so lockout risk and support calls; the comparable small-firm products don't offer it. Firm security in Release 1 = optional two-step sign-in (AG01-3) and one sign-in at a time (AG1-D16). Not a day-to-day feature, so the Release 1 principle is not affected.
- **AG16-6 — Business hours and closed days (option A, amended by the product owner).** Default hours: Monday to Friday, 9:00–17:00, in the firm's timezone; the firm can change them (Settings → Your firm); per-office hours under Advanced with offices. **Closed days are the firm's own list**, maintained by the firm — ExpatReady does not maintain or seed a provincial holiday list (firms differ: one Ontario firm opens on Victoria Day, another closes). The list starts empty; until the firm adds days, every weekday counts as open. **Used only for the firm's internal clocks:** Needs you overdue (AG11-D17), the government-deadline approval lead time, lead response time (AG-02), case target dates (AG-07 BR-12) and bookable slots (AG-04 AG-4-7). *(Case target dates removed 18 Oct — AG07-D27(7): the target filing date counts calendar days.)* **Government and legal dates are calendar dates** (permit expiry, IRCC request or procedural-fairness deadline, biometrics deadline): they are never moved by the firm's hours or closed days. Supersedes AG-04 "holidays seeded from the country pack" (AG-4-7, §13 Q8) and clarifies AG-11 line 492 ("a deadline that lands on a weekend is handled per the firm's calendar"). Code (14 Oct): nothing built; `ERv2-frontend/src/features/settings/company-details/business-hours-tab.tsx` actually renders the mock plan details and goes with the duplicate plan tab.
- **AG16-7 — A reminder or approval due date that falls on a closed day moves earlier, never later (option A).** The government date itself never moves. Reminders and approval due dates counted in working days back from a government date land on the last open day on or before the computed day. Example: permit expires Sun 7 Mar; "2 working days before" → Thu 4 Mar. The file shows the date with its weekday ("Expires Sun 7 Mar (weekend)"). Applies to every government date (Minder, AG11-D17 deadline lead, AG-07, AG-13).
- **AG16-8 — Who changes Escalation and At-risk marks (option A; closes W-10(2)).** Owner and Admin, through the built key `agent/firm-capability:manage` ("Manage the firm's capability switches", `permission-catalog-rows.ts:307-313`; held by firm-owner and firm-admin, `role-template.ts:146, 224`). No reason required; every change goes to the activity log. No new key. Replaces the Team safeguards "Owner only, with a reason" rule (withdrawn with SR-Q1c). The earlier suggestion `agent/settings:edit` does not exist in code and is not used.
- **AG16-9 — Backup approver: removed (option A).** Withdraw AG-16-49 (backup approver and the 5-staff "turn on in 7 days" grace, already gone with SR-Q1c), AG-11 line 526, and the backup field in `FirmTeamSafeguards`. Not built. Covered by AG-11 routing (line 510): a deactivated file owner's items become unassigned and visible to users with `agent/case:assign`; overdue and unassigned items escalate to those users and the Owner; professional approvals show to every holder of the approval permission; AG-01 one-click handover moves a leaver's work. Knock-on for AG-11: line 510 still carries the "preferred" licensed mark, withdrawn by SA-Q3.
- **AG16-10 — Developer API: spec follows code (option A).** AG-16 gets a short section under Advanced → Developer API, documenting what is built in `apps/api/src/developer-api`: API keys (create with a name and optional expiry, shown once, stored only as a hash, revoke at once — `api-key.service.ts:28, 61-73`); read-only search with a key (`external-search.controller.ts:13-14, 58`); webhooks for the 31 published events (leads, cases, documents, deadlines, invoices and payments, signing, bookings — `apps/worker/src/consumers/webhook-dispatch.consumer.ts`), each signed with its own secret, 24-hour overlap on secret change (`webhook-subscription.service.ts:36-44`); address safety check (`webhook-url-guard.ts`); per-key request limit. Owner and Admin only (`agent/developer-api:manage`, `role-template.ts:179, 253`); every change in the activity log; nothing on by default. Shown when the firm's plan includes SA-03 feature #21 "Developer (API and webhooks)", otherwise an upgrade note; which plans include it is a Super Admin plan choice (code tier `enterprise` marked FOUNDER-CONFIRM, `capability.ts:331-337`). No OAuth (client ID and secret) in Release 1; keys only. **For later reviews:** AG-02 — the inbound lead API keys (AG-2-6, not built) share one "API keys" screen with this; phase of an ExpatReady listing in Zapier's app directory (works with key auth). AG-06 — phase of native QuickBooks / Xero sync (now "Phase 2/3", AG-06 line 744; ask whether day-to-day).
- **AG16-11 — One "Automatic messages" page (option A), documented in one place only.** Settings section 6 → **Automatic messages**: one row per automatic client or lead message (every recipe, reminder, chase, follow-up, sequence step, countdown) showing when it sends, a preview, and one choice **Send automatically · Ask me first · Off**. ExpatReady defaults pre-set (most "Send automatically"; going-cold, no-show recovery and post-consultation follow-up "Off"; class-2 "Starts ticked / unticked" kept on the row, AG12-D3). Locked rows show the product floors (one-off AI-written text → always "Ask me first"; an AI-edited template is approved once when published and is not a per-send floor, per R7 — *wording corrected 14 Oct to match R7*). **Removed:** the separate recipe on/off for client-message recipes (AG-11), the Advanced → AI & automation autonomy table (AG-16 §3.9), and any per-module message toggle. Internal actions (tasks, staff alerts, filing) are not on the page. Permission: `agent/template:configure` (SA05-R9b). Not built (no backend or frontend code). **Single source rule (the product owner):** **AG-12 is the only spec home** for the page (rows, choices, defaults, preview, permission). **AG-11** keeps only the engine behaviour (what "Ask me first" does in Needs you; the floors list) and references AG-12 for the page. Every other document refers to "the Automatic messages page (AG-12)" in one line and documents no control of its own. Already withdrawn and to be removed wherever they remain: Supervised client messages and "Who may approve AI-written client text" (R5), Team safeguards (SR-Q1c), backup approver (AG16-9). **Sweep list** (mentions found 14 Oct, applied in each epic's own review; checked again in a final cross-epic consistency pass): AG-11 (56), AG-16 (36), AG-12 (19), AG-02 (12; its "Sequence library — toggle on/off, edit steps" becomes rows on this page; step editing is a question for the AG-02 review against R2 "firm-authored recipes"), AG-10 (12), AG-07 (10), AG-18 (10), AG-15 (7), AG-17 (7), Glossary 99 (7; "Autonomy level", "Standard messages review"), Overview 00 (6), AG-09 (5), AG-08 (2).
- **AG16-12 — Where timings live (option A, with two refinements).** Chase timings (first after 5 days, then every 7, at most 4; limits 1–30 days and 1–10 reminders, AG11-D17) sit **on the Missing-document chase row** of the Automatic messages page (AG-12, AG16-11), shown as one plain sentence with editable numbers: "First reminder **5** days after we ask, then every **7** days, up to **4** times." Every other timed message shows its timing the same way on its own row. The two Needs you timings (overdue after 2 working days; government-deadline approvals 2 working days before) stay under **Advanced → Waiting times** (AG-16). The separate "Reminders page" in AG16-1 section 6 is dropped. Per-client exceptions use the existing per-client pause ("Client paused", Glossary), not firm settings. Pattern matches HubSpot / Clio Grow sequences, Mailchimp journeys and Calendly / Jane reminders (timing on the step). Not built.
- **AG16-13 — "Getting paid" defaults and scenarios (approved with fixes 1–4).** **Single home:** AG-06 owns the invoicing rules; AG-16 only places the screen (Settings section 4). Code (14 Oct): firm invoicing is not built in the backend (`apps/api/src/billing` is ExpatReady's own subscription billing; AG-16 line 229 "exists today" refers to the old system); frontend numbering dialog is a mock (`invoice-configuration-dialog.tsx:31-53`). Already decided in AG-06 and kept: number given at issue, not at draft (AG-6-5); issued invoices immutable, corrections by credit note (AG-6-6); atomic numbering (BR-4).
  - **Numbering:** (1) default **INV-0001**, one sequence for the firm, grows past 9999; (2) **"Next invoice number" editable** so a migrating firm continues (e.g. 2418); (3) Advanced: "Include year and restart each year" → INV-2026-0001, INV-2027-0001; (4) restart is offered **only with the year in the number** (no duplicates); (5) monthly restart (INV-2026-03-001) under Advanced; (6) the year is the invoice date's calendar year — a firm on a non-calendar financial year changes the prefix itself; (7) a deleted draft uses no number; (8) a wrong issued invoice is **cancelled** (keeps its number, shown "Cancelled"), a credit note is issued and the corrected invoice takes the next number — never deleted; (9) a prefix change applies to the next invoice only; (10) a next number lower than one already used is refused ("INV-0042 already exists"); (11) **no hand-typed invoice numbers in Release 1**; (12) Advanced: separate sequence per office, only with a different prefix (TOR-, VAN-); (13) credit notes have their own series **CN-0001**, same rules; (14) payment receipts show the invoice number, no own series; (15) imported old invoices (Release 2, AG18-D3) will keep their original numbers and not affect the sequence; (16) switching from yearly restart to continuous continues from the current number.
  - **Tax:** pre-filled from the **billing party's** address (the client, employer or payer the invoice is addressed to — AG-06 line 170), falling back to the firm's province; e.g. ON HST 13%, QC GST 5% + QST 9.975% with both numbers printed. "Not registered for tax" switch (no tax lines, no number). Billing party outside Canada → one-line hint "Client is outside Canada — check whether tax applies"; the firm decides, never blocked (rule 7). Government-fee lines never taxed (AG17-D13). Default tax added on top; "Prices include tax" under Advanced. ExpatReady keeps a small provincial rate table; a rate change never alters issued invoices. Tax changeable per line on a draft. **One firm tax number** (no per-office tax set-up).
  - **Due date and currency:** default **Due in 14 days**, firm-changeable; **"Due on receipt" one click** per invoice (retainers); payment-plan instalments carry their own dates; overdue reminders are the "Invoice overdue" row on the Automatic messages page (AG16-11). Default currency CAD (AG01-9); the invoice uses the billing party's currency when set (AG-06 line 167).
  - **Language:** invoice produced in the client's language (EN or FR); footer and offline payment instructions held in both. Empty by default; a message needing payment instructions waits until filled (Guard 1).
  - **For the AG-06 review (in this order):** (a) **Refunds are Phase 2 in AG-06** (lines 74-75, 169) — conflicts with the Release 1 principle (refund on withdrawal or refusal is day-to-day) and with (8) above; (b) retainer paid before work is done — client / trust account handling for unearned fees (AG-06 has only an optional funds-nature tag, line 285; rule 7 applies); (c) milestone billing triggered by a case event (e.g. "submitted"), not only a date (AG-06 line 121); (d) small-balance write-off (e.g. $25 lost to wire fees).
- **AG16-14 — Staff notifications: personal settings only (option A).** The firm notification matrix (event × channel × audience grid), firm quiet hours and the firm-as-ceiling rule are **removed** (AG-16 §3.3, AG-16-9, AG-16-10 ceiling part, BR-6, AG-16-33 matrix rows, `FirmNotificationPolicy`). **Who** is told is decided by the product (assignee, file owner, approver), not configured. **My account → Notifications:** about 8 plain groups, each with In-app and Email switches. Defaults: in-app **on** for everything; email **on** for what is mine or urgent (assigned to me, Needs you, my deadlines, payment failed, new lead assigned to me, a client messaged me); email **off** for noisy items (every document upload, every stage change). Security emails (new sign-in, password change) always on, not switchable. No quiet hours. **No digest and no daily summary email** for now (the product owner, 14 Oct); "Done for you" home screen covers the daily view. Notification language follows the user's language (AG-16-11 kept). Permission `agent/notification:manage-preferences` (self) kept. Not built (AG-16 line 829). Pattern: Clio, MyCase, Jane (per-person settings, no firm grid).
- **AG16-15 — Mailboxes: firm and personal, nothing required (option A with fixes 1–5).** **Day one:** email goes through ExpatReady's shared sending with the firm's name and a reply-to (built: `apps/api/src/rails/adapters/email`, `rail-config.resolver.ts`). **Settings → Connections:** the firm may connect one **shared mailbox** (e.g. info@). **My account → Email:** each person may connect **their own** mailbox. Gmail and Microsoft 365 are buttons; **"Other provider (IMAP/SMTP)"** is collapsed (GoDaddy, Zoho Mail and others) — closes the IMAP placement point. **Sending address:** (1) **automatic messages always send from the firm mailbox**, or shared sending if none; a message a person writes sends from their own mailbox if connected, else the firm mailbox, else shared sending. The consent rules by route (G-Q2 / DEV-TASKS B3) apply to the route actually used. (2) An email received in more than one connected mailbox is **filed once** (deduplicated by the email's message ID). (3) **Firm mailbox:** unknown senders go to the Unmatched inbox with "Create lead". **Personal mailbox:** only emails with known clients and leads are brought in; unknown senders are ignored (privacy); the person can file a single email to a client by hand. (4) Deactivating a person **ends their mailbox connection automatically**; emails already filed stay in the client file. (5) Gmail read access needs Google app verification with an annual security assessment, and Microsoft a similar approval — already on the "before go-live" list (SA-08); longest lead-time item, start early. Not built (no firm or personal mailbox code). Pattern: Clio, MyCase, Docketwise (personal mailboxes) and HubSpot (shared inbox). Owner of the mailbox rules: AG-10; AG-16 places the screens.
- **AG16-16 — Get help: who raises and sees tickets (option A).** Every firm user may raise a ticket and sees **their own**; **Owner and Admin see all the firm's tickets** and may reply on any. Get help opens from a **"?" button on every screen** (form opens with the current page noted automatically) and from Settings → Help & support (section 8). Form: one text box plus optional attachments — **closes SA-10 Q7** (the queue shows the first line as the subject; support sets category and priority). Spec home AG-16 (AG01-16). Keys `agent/support-ticket:create` (every starter role) and `agent/support-ticket:list` (own tickets for all; all firm tickets for Owner and Admin) are not in code yet (SA-10 line 496) — to add with SA-07/SA-06 seed parity. Knock-on: SA-10 Q7 closed, SA10-X5 resolved.
- **AG16-17 — Payment account down: money never falls back (option A, full version with refinements 1–4; the product owner chose full over the lean version).** Withdraws AG-16 line 271 "payment falls back to platform processing only if the firm's plan permits" and the "(or hold, for payment per plan)" branch of the data flow (line 281); "if the plan permits" is void since SA-Q1 (payment account on every plan). Client money never passes through ExpatReady (Stripe Connect: funds go to the firm's account). Code: no shared payment processing for firm clients exists; `rails/payment-rail.port.ts` and the Stripe adapter serve ExpatReady's own subscription billing. When the firm's payment account is paused, failed or disconnected: the client's payment page shows **"Online payment is temporarily unavailable"** with the firm's **offline payment instructions** (Getting paid). **(1)** If no offline instructions are filled in, the page shows "Please contact [firm name, phone, email] to arrange payment". **(2)** An instalment due on a saved card **waits**, shown "Waiting — payment account disconnected", and **runs automatically once the account reconnects**; the client gets the normal receipt (the client agreed to the plan; no new consent). **(3)** "Invoice overdue" reminders still send, with the "Pay now" link replaced by the offline instructions (or contact details). **(4)** The firm is alerted at once — in-app, email and a dashboard banner that stays until fixed — driven by the payment provider's account-status notice (connection health, AG-16 Connect → Verify → Health). Email and other connections keep their fallback (AG16-15). Owner of payment behaviour: AG-06; AG-16 owns the connection health and alert.
- **AG16-18 — Calendars: personal only (option A, with "choose which calendars").** The firm-wide calendar connection (AG-16 §3.4 rail 2, "the firm's own Google/Microsoft calendar") is **removed** from Settings → Connections. Each person may connect their own Google or Microsoft calendar in **My account → Calendar** (optional); without it, booking works from the hours they set in ExpatReady. On connecting, the person chooses **which calendar(s) to check for busy times** and **which calendar bookings are added to** — this may be a shared calendar (e.g. a firm "Consultations" calendar), the Calendly pattern. Office closures come from the firm's closed-days list (AG16-6); the team's bookings are seen in ExpatReady's own schedule view; booking confirmation emails carry a calendar invite (covers iCloud / phone-only users). **AG-04 is the single spec home** (its model already ties `CALENDAR_CONNECTION` to `STAFF_MEMBER`, AG-04 line 118). Not built (no calendar code). Pattern: Calendly, Clio, Jane.
- **AG16-19 — No shared ExpatReady WhatsApp number (option A).** WhatsApp works only through the firm's own connected WhatsApp Business account (AG-16 §3.4 rail 4). Without a connection — or without an approved template, or when a template is Paused / Rejected (R9-24, DEV-TASKS C4) — the message goes **by email**. The general "graceful fallback to the platform shared rail" (AG-16 line 271) does **not** apply to WhatsApp (nor to payment, AG16-17); it applies to email only. Reason: clients see only the firm's own number; replies reach the firm; no impersonation risk; simpler Meta approval. Code: only the `messenger.whatsapp` placeholder (`capability.ts:227`).
- **AG16-20 — AI switch details (option A with refinements 3 and 4; Owner-only on, as recommended).** (1) **Only the Owner turns AI on**, once, with a short acknowledgement ("Client documents and messages will be processed by our AI provider in Canada. Nothing is used for training."), audited; **Owner or Admin may turn it off** at any time. (2) ExpatReady supplies optional client-notice wording the firm *may* add to its privacy notice or retainer — never forced (rule 7). (3) **ExpatReady pause** (kill switch or provider terms withdrawn) — closes AG-16 §13 Q7 and line 720: AI helpers stop; banner "AI helpers are paused by ExpatReady. Everything else works as normal."; proposals already made stay for review; staff work manually; AI **resumes automatically only for firms that had it on** — a firm that switched AI off meanwhile stays off; no new acknowledgement. (4) **Provider change** (still Canada, no training, no retention — Super Admin cannot record otherwise, AG11-D15): the Owner is emailed **before the change takes effect**, naming the new provider, so they can turn AI off if they object; no re-acknowledgement. AI plan inclusion is already set by SA-03 catalogue #30 (one sellable extra, one switch; SA-03 line 381) — correction 14 Oct, not "pending". Not built.
- **AG16-21 — Undoing deletes (refined option A).** Code (14 Oct): document delete is permanent (AG-09 line 418, manual `deleteDocument`); no deleted marker on documents, notes or tasks (only `Firm.deletedAt`, `tenancy-core.prisma:157`). Rules: (1) **"Undo"** shown for a few seconds after any delete. (2) **"Recently deleted"** (Settings → Security & your data): documents, notes, tasks and leads restorable for **30 days**, then removed permanently; the deleter, Owner and Admin can restore, back to where it was. (3) A deleted item **leaves the client portal at once**; the bin is staff-only. (4) **"Move to another client or case"** for misfiled documents (privacy case) — AG-09 review to confirm whether a true move exists beyond cross-client linking. (5) **Clients and cases with any history are closed, not deleted** (reopenable, searchable); delete is offered only for an empty client or lead (duplicate, spam; bulk delete for spam leads). (6) **Never deletable:** issued invoices (cancel, AG16-13), sent messages, the activity log. (7) An approved "delete my data" request skips the bin (own 7-day grace and Cancel, G-Q1); items on legal hold cannot be deleted (G-Q5). Replacing a document makes a new version, not a delete (AG-09). No bin settings, no per-item retention, no approval step. Owners: AG-09 (documents), AG-13 (notes, tasks), AG-02 (leads), AG-05/AG-07 (clients, cases); AG-16 places the Recently deleted screen.
- **AG16-22 — One escalation rule: push the high-stakes, show the routine.** Replaces three conflicting rules (Minder deadline escalation, SR-Q1c / AG16-8; overdue-task escalation, AG-13-7 / AG17-D57, opt-in "to the assignee's manager" with a configurable rule; Needs you overdue escalation, AG-11 line 510, always on). **One "Escalation" switch**, on by default, Advanced, Owner or Admin (`agent/firm-capability:manage`, AG16-8). When on, it **alerts everyone who can reassign cases** (`agent/case:assign`) for: (1) **government deadlines** — 2 days before and on the day; (2) **Needs you items overdue** — after 2 working days (AG11-D17). **Overdue tasks are not pushed to others:** the assignee is reminded, and an **"Overdue by person"** list on the team view (AG-13) lets the Owner check anytime. A task linked to a government deadline is covered by the deadline alert — one alert, not two. **No alert to the person who is themselves responsible** (general rule, not solo-only): they already receive their own reminders 2 days before and on the day, so a solo practitioner loses nothing and gets no duplicates; anyone else holding `agent/case:assign` is still alerted. With the switch off, only the responsible person is reminded. Withdrawn: AG-13-7 opt-in task escalation and its "manager" concept and rule builder; AG17-D57 superseded. At-risk marks stay a separate switch (SR-Q1c). Not built. Pattern: Clio, MyCase, Asana (assignee reminders plus overdue lists, no per-task boss alerts).
- **AG16-23 — Client two-step sign-in: no firm "require" switch; client's own choice kept (option B, adjusted).** The built firm policy for the `portal` pool (`mfa-policy-admin.controller.ts:56-63`; `identity-auth.prisma:290-293`) is **not shown** to firms: client two-step is never required by the firm; the code stays dormant (built stays) and can be surfaced later without new code. **Kept:** a client may turn two-step on for themselves in their own portal account (built, `portal-self-mfa.controller.ts`), off unless they choose it; recovery codes as built. With DEV-TASKS C6 (no policy row = not required, both pools) this means clients are never forced. Reason: clients are often abroad, on older phones, not technical, and sign in occasionally — forced two-step causes lockouts near deadlines. Pattern: Clio for Clients (passwordless email link plus device biometrics; two-factor applies to firm users) and MyCase (two-factor documented for staff accounts); no comparable product found that lets firms require it for clients.
- **AG16-24 — Own mailbox and own calendar on every plan (option A; product audit, 14 Oct).** The firm mailbox and personal mailboxes with automatic filing (AG16-15), and personal calendar connections (AG16-18), are **included on every plan**; they are not paid extras. SA-03 catalogue **#27 "Inbox sync" is merged into this** and is no longer sellable. SA-03 **#26 becomes "Own WhatsApp account" only** and stays a sellable extra. Reason: a solo firm on the entry plan must not get double bookings, emails sent from the shared address, or client replies that are not filed (FD-19 "Solo must be complete"; Release 1 principle). Shared sending stays the day-one default until a mailbox is connected (AG16-15). To apply: SA-03 (#26, #27), AG-10, AG-04, AG-16.
- **AG16-25 — Mailbox roles, solo setup, and how email data separates (option A; product audit, 14 Oct; research: `reports/Mailbox connection design for ExpatReady.md`).** Refines AG16-15. (1) **One "Connect your email" button** (onboarding, Settings → Connections, My account → Email); Gmail and Microsoft 365 buttons, Other provider collapsed. (2) **Role set by context:** if the firm has one user, or the Owner connects the first mailbox and no firm mailbox exists, it becomes the **firm mailbox** with no question asked; the screen says "Clients and new enquiries write to this address. Automatic messages will send from it."; "This is my personal address, not the firm's" under Advanced. Anyone else connecting from My account gets a **personal mailbox**. One address has one role at a time. (3) **Second user joins** while the firm mailbox is a named person's address: a one-time notice that staff handling Unmatched will see unknown mail from it, with options "Keep it as the firm email" or "Connect a separate firm address (e.g. info@) and make it my personal email", plus "Decide later". This warns and never blocks. (4) **Personal mailbox "Show me possible new enquiries"**, off by default. When on, unknown-sender emails show in a list **only that person sees**. The list skips the firm's own domain, no-reply senders and bulk mail. Nothing is filed or stored until they click Create lead or File to client; dismissed items are discarded. This list is also the way to "file one email by hand" (AG-10-65). (5) **Separation model:** an email is filed to one record, a lead, client or case (BR-6), and is never tagged to people. Who sees it = whoever can open that record (SA6-D1 open by default, a role can be switched to own-only; AG-7-88 case own = owned or on the case team; AG-2-64 leads). Each email shows the address it came through ("via info@"). Unfiled personal mail is never visible to colleagues and is not stored beyond the message IDs needed to de-duplicate. Connect-time line: "Emails with your clients are visible to colleagues on that client's file. Nothing else is shared." (6) **Who is alerted** when an email is filed: the case officer for a case, the file owner for a client, the lead owner (salesperson) for a lead; not the whole office. (7) Unchanged from AG16-15: shared sending on day one; automatic messages from the firm mailbox, else shared sending; dedupe by message ID; firm-mailbox unknown senders → Unmatched + "Create lead" clicked by a person. Lead conversion keeps the emails (same Contact record, AG-02 §3.0). Not built. Pattern: HubSpot, Pipedrive, Front (separate personal and shared mailboxes, known-contact filter on personal); Copper (suggested people, add or ignore); Front (an individual inbox can be converted to a shared one). No vendor documents a solo flow, so this is our own design. To apply: AG-10 (§3.2a, AG-10-62 to AG-10-67), AG-16 (Connections, My account), AG-02 (lead owner alert).
- **AG16-26 — Email filing order (option A; product audit, 14 Oct).** Replaces the order in AG-10 §3.2 (line 247: reply token to a sent message → address → alias). Rule-based steps, which work with AI off (AG-10-39): (1) **thread** — part of a thread already filed, **sent or received** → same case, even from a new address; (2) **reference in the subject** — ExpatReady file number, IRCC application number or UCI → that case; (3) **address of anyone linked to the case** (client, spouse, dependant, employer HR contact, not only the primary address) → that case **only if exactly one open case** fits; (4) the address fits **several open cases** → Unmatched with those cases suggested, one click to file; (5) no match → Unmatched; on linking, offer "Add this address to [client]'s record?". **Email domain is never used to match people.** Automatic messages carry the file number in the subject (e.g. "Documents needed – MAP-0142"); the per-case plus-address stays as a hidden fallback. **AI on top (existing Doers, no new Doer):** the **Sorter** reads content to rank suggestions in Unmatched (e.g. "job offer for Client B" → Client B's case first) and proposes document types for attachments; the **Reader** finds an IRCC application number inside a PDF letter. An AI-only match is never filed automatically; it goes to Unmatched as a suggestion (BR-22, AG11-D13); all AI is off until the provider is recorded. **Privacy:** the Sorter reads only mail already brought in; unknown-sender mail in a personal mailbox reaches it only if that person has switched on "Show me possible new enquiries" (AG16-25). Pattern: MyCase (address + case number in subject; thread-based linking), Clio, INSZoom (case ID in subject), Lawmatics (unassigned queue). To apply: AG-10 (§3.2, AG-10-10/12, AG-10-37/38), AG-11 (Sorter/Reader inputs), AG-12 (subject line carries file number).
- **AG16-27 — Government mail (IRCC, provincial nominee programs, CBSA): recognise, match, identify the letter, sort, deadline (option A; product audit, 15 Oct).** Evidence: read-only scan of a real firm's general mailbox (14 days, 780 incoming; 25 IRCC emails and 33 PDFs opened). Findings: 58% of incoming mail is government mail; about half of IRCC emails only say "sign in to your account"; 44 manual forwards to staff in 14 days. Notes: `research_notes/Mailbox connection design for ExpatReady/ircc_email_samples.md`, `ircc_pdf_letters.md`, `inbox_mix_snapshot.md`. (1) **Recognise** government mail by **domain** (cic.gc.ca, notification.canada.ca, international.gc.ca, auth.canada.ca, provincial domains such as ontario.ca), from a list ExpatReady maintains, not fixed addresses. It is brought in from **every** connected mailbox, personal ones included, and never ignored. Staff forwards ("Fwd:") are unwrapped, and copies are de-duplicated by the original message and by PDF, so one letter = one item *(changed 17 Oct, AG16-44: every copy is kept; a same-looking copy asks "Same letter / Different letter")*. (2) **Match by rule, without AI:** application number (pattern 1–2 letters + 8–9 digits, e.g. W/S/V/F/E/EP/R/C) in the subject → body → PDF file name → PDF text; then **UCI** (10 digits or `NN-NNNN-NNNN`, hyphens stripped; one application can have several UCIs for family members, so UCI picks the person); then provincial file numbers (e.g. OINP). Otherwise Unmatched, marked "Government mail". (3) **Identify the letter** with a **letter library** maintained by ExpatReady as pack content (Immigration-SME), updatable without a release. Each entry holds the letter type (title or first-sentence phrases; Client-update file names = IRCC form codes, e.g. IMM5756 biometrics, IMM1017 medical, IMM5812 work permit letter of introduction, IMM0276 refusal notes), the standard action, the deadline rule and a suggested client message. Unknown or scanned letters show as "Letter — type not recognised" (the Reader may read scans when AI is on). (4) **Sort into three kinds:** **FYI** (status updated, account linked, receipt) → filed and the case officer is notified, no task; **Action** (new portal message, "waiting to hear from your client", a letter, an information request) → a Tasker task named after the letter for the case officer, e.g. "Biometrics instruction letter — Client B (W30•••••): tell the client, deadline 13 Nov", with one click to share it to the portal or message the client (supports the representative's duty to tell the client; a reminder, never a block); **Noise** (sign-in and one-time codes, GCKey, CRA, newsletters) → not brought in. (5) **Deadline by rule:** "within N days of the date of this letter", "on or before [date]", "seven (7) calendar days" and labelled dates are computed from the letter date. When the anchor is not in the letter (status expiry, PR landing date, date received), the date is taken from the case if stored; otherwise the item shows "Deadline rule found — add the [date]". It is never guessed. The officer confirms before it becomes a government deadline (Minder). (6) **Privacy:** email bodies, subjects and attachment file names are personal data (they can carry names, passport numbers and dates of birth) and are never copied into system logs. Confidence: high for steps 1, 2, 4 and 6 and for the 16 letter types seen; medium for types not yet sampled (passport request, procedural fairness, AOR for PR, COPR, ITA, PNP approvals). Collect 2–3 samples of each from the design-partner firm before build. Fails safe: an unrecognised letter is still filed and creates a task. Doers: Mailer, Tasker, Messenger, Minder (no AI needed); Reader optional. Not built. To apply: AG-10 (§3.2), AG-11 (Reader/Tasker inputs), AG-07 (application number, UCI, provincial file number fields on the case; government deadline), AG-17 (Minder), SA-01/SA-05 (letter library as pack content).
- **AG16-28 — Several firm mailboxes (D5, option A; 15 Oct).** Replaces "at most one firm mailbox" (AG-10-62; `MailboxConnection.kind`, AG-10 line 607). Evidence: an IRCC sender wrote to a second firm Gmail address, not info@ (`ircc_email_samples.md` §3 item 9). Rules: (1) the Owner or Admin may connect **any number of firm mailboxes** (info@, documents@, toronto@), each with firm-mailbox rules (AG16-15, AG16-25: everything brought in; unknown senders → Unmatched). (2) **One is the main address**, "Automatic messages send from this address": the first one connected, changeable in one click. (3) A reply goes out **from the address the email arrived at**. (4) All firm mailboxes feed **one Unmatched list**, each item marked "via documents@". (5) Each firm mailbox may optionally be tied to an **office**, for office-based routing later (queued). (6) Under Advanced: **"This mailbox also receives mail for: …"** lists aliases and Google Group addresses, so mail to those addresses is handled under firm-mailbox rules (header reliability for Group delivery to be tested). Solo firms are unchanged. Not built. To apply: AG-10 (AG-10-62/63, `MailboxConnection` — add `isMain`, `officeId?`, `aliases[]`), AG-16 (Connections).
- **AG16-29 — Who's handling a firm-mailbox email (D6, option B; 15 Oct).** Evidence: clients re-asked after 1–4 days in 4 threads in 14 days; only about 4 human replies were sent from info@ in that time (`inbox_mix_snapshot.md` §3). Applies to firm-mailbox threads; personal-mailbox threads already have an owner. (1) **The owner is set automatically** when the email is filed: the case officer for a case, the file owner for a client, the lead owner for a lead (AG16-25). "Create lead" sets the lead owner. (2) **"Assign to…"** is a small menu item, needed only for an email that can't be filed to a record. (3) **"Waiting on us · N days"** shows on a thread whose last message is from the client or lead, counting calendar days; it resets when anyone replies. (4) **"No reply needed"** clears it (e.g. a "Thanks!" email). (5) Waiting on us for more than **2 working days** (the AG11-D17 clock, counted against the firm's closed days) shows on the owner's home screen and on the Owner's "Overdue by person" view (AG16-22); no extra alerts. **Not added:** a "someone is replying" marker (collisions are rare in a 5–20 person firm and it needs live-update plumbing), ticket numbers, statuses, SLA settings, round-robin. Pattern: Front, Help Scout, HubSpot (assignee + age). Not built. To apply: AG-10 (agent inbox §3.x), AG-13 (Overdue by person), AG-11 (home screen, AG16-40).
- **AG16-30 — Payment emails → suggested payment record (D7, option A; 15 Oct; home AG-06).** Evidence: 98 payment notices (Interac, Stripe, bank) in 14 days; payers are often relatives or employers (`inbox_mix_snapshot.md` §4 item 4). (1) **Recognise** Interac e-Transfer and bank-deposit notices by sender and wording, from a small list ExpatReady maintains. (2) **Suggest a match**, in order: invoice number in the transfer message → payer name matches the client or billing party → a **remembered payer** → exact amount matching exactly one open invoice or instalment. (3) A **"Payments to record"** list on the home screen for holders of the payment-recording permission: "Record $1,500 on INV-0042 (Client B)? [Record] [Pick another invoice] [Not a client payment]". **Nothing is recorded without a click.** (4) After confirming, "Remember Client AF as a payer for Client B?" (pre-ticked). (5) Invoices and payment instructions say "Put INV-0042 in your e-Transfer message". **Stripe notice emails are not brought in**; the firm's own Stripe webhook already records those payments (AG-06 §3.3), so there are no duplicates. Unknown bank formats get no suggestion and are recorded by hand as today. Not built. To apply: AG-06 (§3.4 offline payments, new list, `remembered payer` on the client), AG-10 (recognising notices), AG-11 (home screen, AG16-40).
- **AG16-31 — Standing rule: ExpatReady suggests, the firm decides (the product owner, 15 Oct).** For every automatic feature in mail, letters and payments: ExpatReady may **file** (undoable) and **suggest**; a **person confirms** anything that records money, sets a government deadline, or takes a legal step. **The source is always shown** (the sentence in the letter, the payment notice). Screens say **"Suggested"** until a person confirms, never "Deadline set" or "Payment recorded". Client messages follow the firm's choice on the Automatic messages page (AG-12). ExpatReady never holds client money (AG-06 §3.3). Unrecognised items fail safe (filed, no guess). The disclaimer that suggestions are aids the firm verifies belongs in the terms of service, not on screens (Rule 7: no regulator citations in product text). Applies to AG16-26, AG16-27, AG16-30 and later items.
- **AG16-32 — Lead notification emails become leads (D8, option A; 15 Oct; home AG-02).** AG-02 already covers manual entry with a live duplicate check, its own form widget, the inbound API and Zapier (AG16-10), CSV import, referral, WhatsApp, booking, attribution, merge (§3.2) and the lead response clock (`AG-02…:247-262`). Gap, from the real mailbox: the firm's existing website form, chat widget and Facebook lead ads email notifications to info@ (64 form notifications in 14 days, sent from the firm's own address to itself; about 20 chat transcripts; 3 Facebook lead-ad emails). (1) When Unmatched holds repeated notifications from one form tool or address, ExpatReady offers **"These look like website-form notifications. Always create leads from them?"** (2) The **Owner or Admin clicks Yes once** per sender. From then on each notification creates a lead with source = that form. Because a person set the rule, BR-32 ("a person creates the lead") is respected. (3) Fields are read by rule ("Name:", "Email:", "Phone:", "Message:"), and anything not understood goes into the lead's note. With AI on, the **Sorter** reads any layout, chat transcripts and Facebook emails included (AG16-33). (4) ~~Leads are merged on email or phone~~ *(corrected 17 Oct, AG16-44)*: a match on email or phone shows **"Possible duplicate of [name] — [Link] [Merge] [Keep separate]"** and staff decide; never a silent merge (AG-02 §3.2, `AG-02…:279`). (5) The lead response clock (AG16-6) and the speed-to-lead message start from the notification time. Moving to ExpatReady's own widget or a Meta Lead Ads connection (API or Zapier) stays optional. Not built. To apply: AG-02 (§3.1 new source "form notification email", §3.2), AG-10 (Unmatched suggestion), AG-16 (the per-sender rule list under Connections → firm mailbox).
- **AG16-33 — Standing rule: AI reads, rules check, a person confirms; core works without AI (the product owner, 15 Oct).** Rules are the **floor on every plan**: hard identifiers (thread, file / application / invoice number, exact address, government domain, known letter types, standard form layouts). Where AI is available and switched on, the existing Doers widen coverage, with no new Doer: the **Sorter** (emails — content-ranked matches, any form layout, fuzzy payer names, new bank formats, urgent / enquiry / action tags) and the **Reader** (documents — unseen letter types, scanned letters, deadlines in any wording). AI output is always a **suggestion with its source**; AI-only matches never file on their own (BR-22); money, deadlines and legal steps are confirmed by a person (AG16-31). ExpatReady staff may use AI internally to draft new letter-library and notice-format entries from de-identified unknown items (F4). Where a plan lacks AI, the screen may show a small locked hint, e.g. "Letter type not recognised — included on higher plans". Applies to AG16-26, AG16-27, AG16-30, AG16-32 and later items.
- **AG16-34 — AI is included in higher plans, not sold as an add-on (the product owner, 15 Oct).** Supersedes SA-03 catalogue #30 "extra · Sellable" (`SA-03…:382`) and the AG16-20 note "AI plan inclusion is already set by SA-03 #30". The AI Doers (Reader, Sorter, Writer, Checker; one switch) are a **plan feature included in upgraded plans**. Entry plans, including most solo firms, run on the rules floor (AG16-33); this is acceptable because AI is good to have, not a must. Which plans include it is set in the SA-03 plan builder, not here. Unchanged: AI stays locked until the provider terms are recorded (AG11-D13), and the Owner switches it on once with an acknowledgement (AG16-20). The "AI notice" Must template (SA-3-66(c)) now applies to firms whose plan includes AI and whose Owner has switched it on. To apply: SA-03 (#30 kind and note, SA-3-66(c)), AG-16 (§ AI switch wording), AG-11 (AG11-D4 plan note).
- **Queued after AG16-34 (product audit, 15 Oct; decide one at a time):** ~~D5 more than one firm mailbox~~ (AG16-28); ~~D6 who's handling it~~ (AG16-29); ~~D7 payment notices~~ (AG16-30); ~~D8 leads from notification emails~~ (AG16-32); ~~D9 unauthorised sender~~ (AG16-35); ~~D10 email → task, remind if no reply~~ (AG16-36); ~~D11 go-live essentials~~ (AG16-37); ~~D12 lower-volume items~~ (AG16-38); ~~D13 Translate and Summarise~~ (AG16-39);
- **AG16-39 — "Translate" and "Summarise thread" in Release 1 on plans that include AI (D13, option A; the product owner's release call, 16 Oct).** Both use the existing **Writer** (no new Doer) and show only where the firm's plan includes AI and AI is switched on (AG16-34, AG16-20). (1) **"Translate"** on an incoming email shows an English translation **under the original**, for staff only; it is never sent and the original stays visible. (2) **"Summarise thread"** shows 3–5 lines at the top (what the client asked, what was promised, what is pending), staff only, with the source thread right there. (3) Replying in the client's language is unchanged: the Writer drafts and a person approves AI-written client text (AG11-D1); templates are per language (BR-8). Reason for Release 1: day-to-day reading aids, not client-facing AI content, and they keep client data out of public translation tools. Evidence: 14% of client contact was non-English (French) in the sample firm; other firms' clients write in more languages. Not built. To apply: AG-10 (thread actions), AG-11 (Writer tasks), 99-Glossary (Writer entry).
- **AG16-40 — The home screen is the one place to start (F1, option A; 16 Oct).** Builds on AG11-D7 (home = Needs you, then Done for you; `AG-11…:39, :553`) and keeps AG17-D61's names ("My Tasks"; never "My Work" or "My Day", `AG-17…:181`). Home screen sections, in order: (1) **Needs you** (approvals); (2) **My Tasks — due today or overdue** (including government-letter tasks, AG16-27); (3) **Waiting on us · No reply** (AG16-29, AG16-36); (4) **Payments to record**, shown only to holders of the payment-recording permission (AG16-30); (5) **Unmatched**, shown only to holders of `agent/communication:resolve-unmatched`; (6) **Done for you** (last 24 h).
